# Journal (autopilot)

Newest last. What was tried, what happened, dead ends.

## 2026-10-14
- Mojo 1.1.0 via pixi (`pixi add --pypi mojo`; needed glibc 2.34 on the platform entry).
- Probe: `OwnedDLHandle().get_function[T]("name")` calls one C symbol with any argument types (labs, fabs) ✅. A Mojo `def … abi("C")` passed to C works as a callback (qsort) ✅.
- Draft `apple/hello_mac.mojo` compiled for `arm64-apple-macosx14.0` first try; needed Mojo runtime (`KGEN_*`, 10 functions) because of Mojo's `main`.
- Probe: export a Mojo entry function instead of `main` → only 3 runtime functions left. `@export("main")` is refused ("only 'main' can be exported as 'main'").
- Dead end: defining `KGEN_CompilerRT_AlignedFree` in the same module that uses it → "Call parameter type does not match function signature" (Int vs ptr). Fix: separate Mojo file.
- `KGEN_CompilerRT_GetStackTrace(ptr buf, i64 depth) -> i64` (read from LLVM IR); returns 0 = "no trace". Needed only because errors can be raised.
- Result on Linux: Mojo-only executable, no Mojo runtime, depends only on libc. Prints "Hello, Ada! 3".
- `xtool new --skip-setup` shows xtool's package shape; `xtool install <ipa>` installs a prebuilt app.
- `@export` in the same module as a call to the runtime fn fails → `apple/mojo_rt.mojo` on its own.
- Linked the Mac app with `ld64.lld` + `.tbd` stubs: 72 KB arm64 Mach-O, LC_MAIN → `_mojo_entry`, loads only AppKit, libobjc, libSystem.
- `rcodesign verify` fails on every ad-hoc signature ("CMS error", and it warns its verify is buggy). Wrote `scripts/verify_signature.py` (recomputes page + Info.plist hashes); tamper tests catch a 1-byte change in code and in Info.plist.
- Linker's own ad-hoc signature left 5 KB of stray bytes after rcodesign re-signed → link with `-no_adhoc_codesign`, sign once.
- Read Mojo's `std/ffi/__init__.mojo` (github modular/modular, `Mojo/stdlib/std/ffi`): `_DLCallable` just bitcasts the address to `def(*a: *T) thin abi("C") -> R`. Copied the trick: `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT=-2, "objc_msgSend")` + bitcast → one variadic `msg()`; no dlopen/dlclose, nothing raises, GetStackTrace no longer pulled in.
- Gotcha: printing a Float64 pulls in `KGEN_CompilerRT_fprintf`. Don't print floats in app code (or add a stand-in).
- Disassembly check (arm64): window init puts the rect in d0–d3 (0, 0, 460.0, 280.0) and style/backing/defer in x2/x3/x4 — same as Apple's NSRect-by-value convention. Insets: 32.0 in d0–d3.
- Reviewer: PASS, nothing expected to crash; 10 findings. Fixed 1–9, #10 not an issue (xtool prefixes bundle IDs per user).
- Quirk: Mojo built fine with an unfinished `def broken(` appended to an *imported* module (`hello_core.mojo`); it seems to parse imported modules lazily. Errors in the app file itself fail the build (exit 1), and `build.sh` now stops and leaves no stale objects. The M1 tests cover `hello_core`.
- Resource seal (`_CodeSignature/CodeResources`) now verified too; tamper test caught.
- Re-review: PASS. SDK-26 marking judged low risk (no launch check on dev installs; app already uses scenes).

## Goal 3: web, components, todo, Android, GPU (2026-10-15, owner asleep)
- Mojo Python module (`PythonModuleBuilder`, `PyInit_mojoui_app`) built from the unchanged hello app: Python got the same patches the native hosts get. First try.
- chrome-devtools-axi wants Google Chrome at /opt/google; only /usr/bin/chromium here → Playwright (`pixi add --pypi playwright`) with `executable_path=/usr/bin/chromium`, headless. Clicked Greet 3x → "Hello, Ada! (click 3)".
- Events with values (`Event(message, text, number)`) + 7 components: 14 core tests pass first try.
- GTK: `g_signal_connect_data` needed two callback signatures → `ccall()` looks C functions up with dlsym and casts per call (same trick as objc msg).
- Bug: after Reset, typing stopped reaching update(). Trace: the field's own "changed" signal fired re-entrantly while `apply()` ran, and `self.updating = True` wasn't visible to it (Mojo `mut self` is exclusive; the store can be optimised away). Fix: busy flag kept on the GApplication via g_object_set_data (opaque C calls), checked before handlers touch the host.
- Web components: plain HTML controls + HTMX; text field inside a tiny form so Return submits with no custom JS. Playwright `has_text` regex failed on pretty-printed label HTML → match the label's <span>.
- AppKit + UIKit hosts for every component compile; only Apple system functions. iOS dropdown = UIButton menu (showsMenuAsPrimaryAction + changesSelectionAsPrimaryAction) whose UIActions call a hand-built global Objective-C block (Mojo invoke function); identifier "<tag>:<index>".
- Todo app: same 10 steps in GTK and Chromium → identical state reports.
- GTK CRITICAL g_object_notify_by_pspec: rebuilding inside the dropdown's own notify handler destroyed it mid-signal → rebuilds deferred to g_idle_add; test steps run pending GTK work between steps. Screenshot after a deferred rebuild was empty → wait ~300 ms of real frames.
- GPU: plain `mojo` ships only std; `pixi add --pypi max` (26.6) gives `max.gpu` + `layout`. Modular's Mandelbrot example ran on the RTX 3090 first try.
- Generic per-pixel kernel: kernel params must be `thin` functions; `Int` isn't DevicePassable → Int32 sizes. 480×320 in ~0.1 ms, identical to CPU.
- First fractal was all black: the camera glided through the set's interior → fixed centre on a spiral. GtkPicture grew past its pixels → content-fit scale-down + centre.
- Review (goal 3, round 1): NEEDS WORK — web lost a task on instant Return (reproduced by reviewer), CDN JS, .sesskey committed, checker weaker than goal 2's, etc. All fixed (D-34, D-35); new `enter:` step reproduces the race; harsher todo run identical on GTK and web.
- htmx inherits hx-vals from ancestors: the field's input inherited the form's second=1 → typing = submit. M6 still passed because the shape-version check rejected the duplicate; added a draft-only final step so M6 catches it too.
- GPU memory per web tab measured with nvidia-smi: 512 MiB at 1, 2, 5 and 10 sessions → MAX shares one CUDA context per process; no per-tab cost.

## Goal 6: GPU Data Lens (2026-10-15)
- Probe: Mojo 1.1 has no wasm target ("No available targets are compatible with triple wasm32-*"); a trivial export's LLVM IR retargets with system `llc -mtriple=wasm32` after stripping target attributes. Parked for a later goal.
- Probe: `from std.atomic import Atomic, Ordering` (not std.os.atomic). 20M points binned into 1920×1080 with relaxed atomics: 2.4 ms on the RTX 3090.
- Probe: `max.gpu` imports compile for arm64-apple-macosx/ios; guarding the GPU calls with `comptime if CompilationTarget.is_linux()` removes every AsyncRT symbol from the iOS object (is_macos() alone left them in iOS).
- `comptime` List tables can't be indexed at run time ("cannot materialize… not ImplicitlyCopyable") → `materialize[HUBS]()` once, passed in.
- PointCloud: grid-stride bin kernel (one thread many points, totals added once per thread) + per-pixel shade + one copy into the host surface: 1.48 ms per 1080p frame for 20M points, pixel-identical to the CPU path (130 ms).
- GTK canvas: GtkPicture (can-shrink, content-fit fill) + GdkMemoryTexture per draw, tick callback per canvas; the first draw happened before Hyprland tiled the window, so "drawn" now also means "at the current size".
- Empty MOJOUI_TEST with a screenshot didn't report (empty = unset) → test mode is "MOJOUI_TEST is set".
- GTK playing: 180 frames in 3048 ms = 59.0 fps, every frame redrawn (60 Hz display, 1123×1402 px canvas).
- Web: the driver's readiness probe fetched "/", and every retry started a 20M-point session (3.3 s, ~640 MB each) → the server drowned. The probe now fetches a static file.
- `pkill -f <pattern>` inside a Bash tool command kills the tool's own shell (the pattern is in its command line): use `ps | grep "[w]eb.py"` instead.
- Web frames: 6.6 ms server-side at 1280×800 (draw + JPEG). First measurement 18.8 fps: the loop awaited the side-panel refresh. Measured properly (per-frame intervals): one 371 ms gap as Play starts (click → event → first pull), then 7–10 ms per frame; unpaced, the browser pulled 145 fps, more than any screen shows. Now: side-panel refreshes go alongside (≤ 10/s, always a last one), frames are paced by requestAnimationFrame, the driver skips 10 warm-up frames → 59.9 fps steady.
- Apple: a struct of two doubles returned through `msg[CGPoint]` is read from d0/d1 after the objc_msgSend call (arm64 disassembly: `blr x19; fmadd d0, d0, d2, d1`), matching Apple's CGPoint ABI; CGRect is the same rule with d0–d3.
- AppKit's build() has stacks in its final `else:`, so a new kind's branch must go before it (first attempt: "unexpected token" on the elif after else).
- Lens for Mac/iPhone: new undefined symbols were only libSystem's sin, cos, __sincos_stret (trip generator) and clock_gettime_nsec_np (frame timing); added to the stubs + allowlist (D-61). No AsyncRT/MAX symbols: the GPU path compiles out.
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK, 1 must-fix): a 409 carrying pending changes was ignored by the canvas adapter, and the server kept sending frame events while a rebuild waited. Fixed both. New guard test (`tests/frame_rebuild`, in M6): frames keep coming after a frame-driven rebuild. Honest note: the old code also passed it, because the throttled side-panel refresh (added for the fps fix) happened to deliver the rebuild; the freeze needed timing the test can't force.
- Also from the review: pending patches cleared on every rebuild sent; GTK touchpad scroll is in pixels (gtk_event_controller_scroll_get_unit), now 100 px = 2×; idle canvases stop ticking (GTK tick callbacks removed when idle and re-armed by patches/window resizes; Apple display links paused and woken by patches and size notifications); iPhone pan limited to one finger; GPU failures are printed and switch the cloud to the CPU; GPU buffers grow together and are reused (no reallocation per filter change); grid size no longer the 3090's SM count; CGImageCreate's bool/int32 stack arguments at their natural sizes; README claims qualified.
- Full check after round 1: M2 failed twice over. (a) Real bug from the idle change: Hyprland resized the window after the first draw and nothing re-armed the canvas (GTK4 doesn't update default-width on compositor resizes) → listen to the GdkSurface "layout" signal, and stop ticking only after 3 idle ticks (layout runs after tick callbacks in a frame). (b) Environment: the desktop now tiled the test window into a 661-pt slot (canvas 321 pt < 400) → GTK test windows are non-resizable at the app's own size (Hyprland floats them), so measurements don't depend on the desktop.
- Review round 2 (NEEDS WORK): the guard test couldn't fail, because every frame changed a label and the canvas version, so the throttled refresh always delivered the rebuild. Now nothing changes while playing except the frame-driven rebuild: with the JS fix removed, `wait:Replay` times out (red); with it, it passes (green). Also: events against a stale screen while a rebuild waits now get the new screen instead of acting on the wrong control; GTK re-arms on the surface's scale change and AppKit on NSWindowDidChangeBackingPropertiesNotification; iPhone canvases wake from `layoutSubviews` (calling UIImageView's own via objc_msgSendSuper) instead of orientation notifications; failed GPU uploads are printed.
- iOS objects after goal 6: still only the four KGEN runtime functions (AlignedAlloc, AlignedFree, GetStackTrace, fprintf), all defined in mojoui/hosts/mojo_rt.mojo. Review round 3: PASS. Final full check 9/9.

## Goal 7: Android (2026-10-15)
- Owner ran scripts/android_setup.sh (licences accepted by them). Installed the emulator + android-36 default x86_64 image with sdkmanager: no licence prompt appeared (covered by the accepted ones).
- avdmanager wrote the AVD under ~/.config/.android/avd (XDG); the emulator only looks in ~/.android/avd → ANDROID_AVD_HOME. `-d pixel_7` failed (no devices.xml in the image) → plain AVD + lcd settings in config.ini.
- Mojo targets x86_64-linux-android29 as well as aarch64; both libraries go in one APK.
- First run: the hello APK started ("HelloMojo: started, 5 nodes" in logcat), showed a TextView/Button screen, and 3 real `input tap`s gave "Hello, Ada! (click 3)" (uiautomator dump).
- Mojo borrow checker: passing self.canvases[k].pixels to a `mut self` method is aliasing → copy first.
- Todo first run: squeezed to 204 px and one row 1924 px tall: the root's fixed width (320) was ignored (WRAP) → root LayoutParams take the root's own size; justify gaps are 0×0 with weight 1.
- Slider: a tap on the thumb itself doesn't move it, so nudging by a few pixels never converged (73 instead of 70) → calibrate with two far-apart taps, step away from the thumb, tap the computed spot.
- Lens on Android: Mojo's perf_counter_ns() compiled to Apple's clock_gettime_nsec_np for Android too (dlopen failed) → mojoui/clock.mojo (clock_gettime on Android, -D MOJOUI_ANDROID). The compiler prints "no libcall available for fsincos" for Android targets but still emits separate sin/cos calls (harmless; the symbol check proves it).
- First full check: M6's "≥ 500 colours" was miscalibrated for 2M points on the CPU (a few dozen density levels: 39 colours) → measure painted share + gradient instead (D-70). M7: README linked docs/report.md, moved to docs/goals/06-report.md (goal 7's report restores it).
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK): radio/tab option ids (j+1) collided with the group's own id (findViewById matches the group first) → options use 1,000,000 + j; M5 now switches tabs for real. That exposed a bigger bug: with targetSdk 36, Android 15+ draws edge to edge, so tall centred screens put their top under the status/action bars, unreachable by touch → setFitsSystemWindows on the content view, and flexible space instead of centring gravity.
- Also from the review: JNI exceptions logged under the mojoui tag (the driver's crash scan sees them); strings cross JNI as UTF-16 (modified UTF-8 breaks emoji); cursor positions in UTF-16 units; canvases reuse one Bitmap filled straight from the pixels through a direct ByteBuffer (no colour conversion: same byte layout); a canvas keeps its gestures from scrolling parents; touch cancel handled; the host is freed in onDestroy. Freeing the app pulled in MAX's DeviceBuffer/DeviceContext release functions through PointCloud's (always empty) GPU fields → two no-op stand-ins for CPU-only builds (mojoui/hosts/no_gpu_rt.mojo), like mojo_rt.mojo.
- M5 after the edge-to-edge fix: the layout example no longer fits one screen and uiautomator reports only on-screen views → M5 measures at 300 dpi (more dp on screen); the rules are in dp, so unchanged.
- Review round 2 suggestion setPremultiplied(false) crashed: "Canvas: trying to use a non-premultiplied bitmap" (views only draw premultiplied bitmaps) → reverted; translucent canvas pixels on Android are a noted limit.
- Final full check: 8/8 in one run. Review round 2: PASS.
- GPU group-by: global atomics only: 22 ms for 20M rows into 5 groups (contention on 10 addresses). Per-block totals in shared memory (stack_allocation + barrier, std Atomic works on shared pointers), flushed once per block: 1.1 ms, still exact.

## Goal 8: analytics showcase (2026-10-15)
- Probes: 64-bit atomic adds on the GPU are exact (20M rows, 64 groups, 1.9 ms). MX frame 0.6.0 from PyPI runs in this env; its native executor needs libKGENCompilerRTShared.so on the loader path (a Mojo app/module has already loaded it, so inside our apps it's found).
- Text: hinted 26 px advances drifted 4.3 px over a heading → advances from the unhinted 1000-unit font: 0.04 px.
- GPU group-by: global atomics 22 ms for 5 groups → per-block shared-memory totals (stack_allocation SHARED + barrier) 1.1 ms.
- GTK: GtkPicture reports its texture's pixel size as its natural size → fixed-height canvases stretched on a 1.6× screen and drew blurry → canvas pictures sit in a GTK_POLICY_EXTERNAL scrolled window (takes no size from the child). Fixed-width views now refuse hexpand passed up from children (the sidebar drifted). Dark desktop theme made card text white on white → FLUENT forces the light scheme.
- "Flint" first read as Fluent (D-73); the owner sent https://microsoft.github.io/flint-chart/ → Flint is the chart language (D-76). The npm release (0.5.1) has no validateChart export yet → the check compiles each spec through assembleVegaLite, then Vega-Lite → Vega → SVG.
- The gallery's side-by-side showed Flint's chart in cents → Flint JSON now carries dollars for money fields.
- Engines on 20M rows, 7 aggregations: GPU 5.9 ms, MX frame (Python + native CPU executor) 114 ms, Mojo loops 746 ms; equal to the cent.
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK): fixed()'s negatives (-5.3 → "-6.8"); month-on-month with a year picked (the month query had filtered last year out → months ignore the year, chart slices; D-78); app logic and Flint export untested → tests/test_analytics.mojo (calendar, crossfilter rules, Flint dollars, mid-chart taps, and all 910 results of the three engines compared under 5 filter settings; built as a binary: `mojo run`'s JIT clashes with MX frame's runtime: "AsyncRT::getOrCreateCPUDevice … different options"). Flint check: warnings fail, labels must appear in the SVG, encoded fields must exist (a spec naming a missing field had passed Flint 0.5.1). Benchmark covers 200 groups (global path) and counts. No-scroll covers the sidebar, main column and page scroll size. MX frame bridge embedded (works from any directory); failures surface with the reason instead of zeros. Docs: timings as ranges (6–9 ms), platform claims marked unverified, per-tab memory limit stated. Screenshots retaken.

- Final full check: 9/10. M9's two failing sub-checks are goal 6's lens checks: (a) 44 fps on a loaded machine (goal 7's own build measured 27–32 fps at the same moment, 59 fps earlier today); (b) the web lens's colour-count 'not blank' test was calibrated on JPEG noise; exact PNG still frames have ~40 colours on a drawn map (42% painted). Not changed: proposed to the owner (painted share, like D-70). Review round 2: one must-fix (engine test didn't prove each engine ran) → asserts added; then fixed.

## Goal 9: the Workbook (2026-10-15, night run)
- Set up: goal 8 archived to docs/goals/08-*; GOAL.md M1–M10; scripts/check.py chains 08 → 07 → …; steps for the morning walkthrough fixed up front (EDIT/PIVOT/CHART/FORMULA/MOVE).
- Owner's mid-flight input: shared read-only data across web sessions (M8) before the wasm spike; folded into the frame design (overlay + virtual formulas, D-80).
- M1: `mojoui/frame.mojo` + `tests/test_frame.mojo` pass (built as a binary). Dead end on the way: Mojo 1.1's compiler segfaults (exit 139, no message) on a `SIMD[int64, 8]` used as an interpreter stack with a run-time index; found by bisecting with 20 probe files built in parallel; `stack_allocation[8, Int64]()` works and is GPU-safe. Noted in CLAUDE.md.
- Semantics settled: `cents` is money (scale 2), so `cents // 50` means "bins of $50"; constants carry their own scale ("0.15" → scale 2); `/` always yields scale 2; `*` of two decimals is rescaled back to 2.
- M2: `mojoui/frame_gpu.mojo`. First kernel (a 16-wide row vector built per row + a stack_allocation stack) took 23 ms per query; reading columns straight from the row and keeping the interpreter's stack in four registers brought it to 0.8–1.9 ms on 20M rows (goal 8's compile-time kernel: 1.1 ms). Programs deeper than 4 are refused at formula time (`program_depth`). GPU = CPU on 262 numbers, 7 query shapes; a session's edits, removals and appended rows reach the next group-by as deltas; undo restores.
- M3/M4: `examples/workbook/app.mojo` + Board on both hosts. Dead ends: (1) GTK test steps ran before the first layout pass inside a GtkFixed, so taps hit a 0×0 canvas → `wait_allocated()` and "drawn once" before canvas/board steps; (2) GTK's chart hit used sizes from the last draw, which hadn't happened yet → same fix; (3) a tap at 30% of a `.grow()` canvas landed on different rows per host (the edit bar wraps differently) → the grid canvas has a fixed height; (4) the summary showed "$3.81B", so a $7 edit was invisible → the exact total, every cent; (5) GTK and the web report widgets in a different order → the checker compares sorted states; (6) the drive CLI's default 900-px viewport puts the right-hand chart off-screen (the checker passes 1440×900).
- M5/M6/M8: `mojoui/workbook.mojo` (a folder: workbook.json + Parquet through pyarrow), Save/Open in the app, `examples/workbook/contoso.mxwb` written by the app itself, `scripts/workbook.sh`, `docs/morning.md`, gallery and README. The Linux binary must run under `pixi run` (the Parquet writer imports numpy from the pixi environment). A Mojo struct holding a Frame can't be partly moved out of ("destroyed out of the middle"): `open_workbook` returns the Frame and fills `mut` out-parameters instead. The analytics app now builds its 20M rows once per process (`init_shared`), so the web host serves every tab from one GPU copy; the check opens two tabs and reads "SHARED: 1 load, 2 sessions" from the server's log.
- M9 (wasm spike, about an hour): Mojo 1.1 refuses wasm triples, but `--emit llvm` + retargeting the IR to wasm64 (scripts/wasm_convert.sh) + LLVM 22's llc + wasm-ld works. A stdlib sample (struct, List, String) has 8 undefined symbols; `spike/wasm_shim.c` (25 lines: bump allocator + stdout calls) covers them, and hello, List/String and print samples run in headless Chromium (scripts/wasm_run.py). Dead end on the way: `write` declared with a 64-bit fd in Mojo → a wasm signature mismatch trap; the shim takes `long`. Verdict: shim-sized (docs/wasm.md).
- M10: README reopened picture-first (four showcase images, badges, the platform diagram and a build/run table in the first 60 lines).
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK, 4 must-fix): (1) the formula compiler scaled the wrong operand whenever the right-hand side had more than one term (`q + cents * q` → `q + cents·100·q`): the insertion point for the left operand's SCALE is now the right operand's start, passed explicitly; tests with hand-computed values for `+ *`, `/ (…)`, `min(a, b - c)`, `(a - b) * (c + 1)`. (2) The stack-depth limit applied per formula, not to the flattened program the kernel runs: now checked on the flattened code; a formula built on formulas is in the GPU test; replacing a formula recompiles its dependants. (3) The in-app Save/Open check passed even when Open failed: it now asserts "Opened" and that the summary equals the edited one; the app opens the sample workbook at start (M6 as written). (4) Add row → Undo left the selection on a row past the end: `alive()`/`set()` reject rows ≥ rows(), the app drops the selection and the sort order. Also: pan keeps its sub-row remainder; Open resets the GPU frame to the shared one; the source name carries the real row count; workbook files are bounds-checked and control characters escaped; the shared registry key carries the PID; the web list layout allows touch scrolling and only headers drag; session start runs under the engine lock; the analytics app is run with two tabs; README images are counted without badges; docs/wasm.md tempered (7 shim symbols + the linker's; evidence, not proof).
- M7's lens timeout was mine, not the load: `wait_allocated()` (added for GTK test taps) ran `g_main_context_iteration` in blocking mode, and goal 6's plain test runs (no MOJOUI_SHOT, window never shown) have nothing pending, so the first canvas step hung until the 120 s timeout. Both waits are non-blocking now and give up when nothing is pending; an unshown lens run takes 3.5 s again. Lesson: a test-only wait must never block on events that an unshown window can't produce.
- M7, three rounds: (1) the blocking test wait (above); (2) goal 8's `flint_specs` and `test_analytics` build the analytics app with no host, so `get_shared(10)` was 0 → the apps now call `init_shared()` themselves when nothing did; `test_analytics` follows the `table()` accessor; (3) goal 5's rule that apps import only `mojoui` caught `std.memory`/`std.math`/`std.os` in the apps → `mojoui.shared.environment()` and `mojoui.maths.log2`. Left: goal 6's lens fps (21–42 fps under a load of 9–12; 59 idle) and its colour-count proxy, both the owner's call.
- Morning feedback (2026-10-16): "how do I add a card?", "how do I edit a cell?", "sort becomes slow or crashes", "moving the cards is jittery". Sort: `permutation()` sorted 20M `KeyIdx` structs with the standard sort, quadratic on low-cardinality keys → a radix sort over values gathered straight from the shared column (overlay applied to changed rows only); bench in tests/bench_frame.mojo (D-91). Jitter: the GTK drag gesture sat on the card it moved, so every `gtk_fixed_move` shifted the gesture's own coordinate frame → one gesture on the Board, hit-tested on drag-begin (D-92); the drop then rebuilt the whole page (every canvas re-created) → a SET_PLACE patch on both hosts (D-93); the first web attempt returned a `<script>` which htmx never ran under `swap: 'none'` → a hidden marker element the Board adapter applies. Cards: an Insert… menu and Remove buttons (D-94); the first try (four buttons) wrapped the bar and the check caught the note's corner off-screen. Checks: M3 now covers Sort (both hosts, under 60 s with start-up), Insert and Remove; M4 the move/resize as before; drive scrolls an object into view before dragging it, and reads only `.object` children of a board.
- Headless checks (D-95): `pixi add xorg-xvfb-server xkeyboard-config` (Linux only; the pixi manifest's named platform is `linux-glibc-234`). Xvfb needs an xkb directory (`-xkbdir /usr/share/X11/xkb`, or pixi's `share/xkeyboard-config-2`); GTK under Xvfb segfaults with its GL renderer on this NVIDIA box (MESA-EGL "Could not get DRI3 device") → `GSK_RENDERER=cairo GDK_DISABLE=gl,vulkan`. The owner's `GDK_SCALE=2` doubles the window, so the virtual screen is 3840×2400. Lens: 180 frames at 60.0 fps headless; workbook M3/M4 pass headless. Also fixed: a `Spacer()` in a card header took the card's spare height on GTK (the chart canvas shrank to a strip) → headers use `.justify(BETWEEN)`.
- Headless, second pass: at the desktop's GDK_SCALE=2 the lens canvas is 1880×1600 px and cairo manages 27 fps (36 with Mesa's software GL); at scale 1 both renderers give 60.0 fps → the headless display runs at scale 1 (`scripts/headless.py`). Goal 8's analytics screenshot has 1275–1335 colours headless (the page is complete: screenshot checked) against its ≥ 1500 bar, which was calibrated on the desktop's NVIDIA GL renderer with subpixel text; forcing `gtk-xft-rgba=rgb` through a settings.ini changed nothing. Not lowered: the same kind of proxy as goal 6's "not blank" count, listed for the owner with it. A standalone `docs/goals/06-check.py M3` run rebuilds goal 6's examples without their APKs (its main ignores arguments), which is what made goal 7's "no APK" failures: rebuilt with `scripts/build.sh <ex> android`.
- Headless, third pass: on X11 a shown test window's first layout waits for the server's map event and each step's layout for the next frame-clock tick (a timer, not "pending" work), so goal 5's layout check measured 0-wide columns → the GTK test driver blocks for the first allocation and lets the frame clock tick once after every step (`wait_frame`, shown windows only). Goal 6's 39 fps in the chain was the Android emulator (booted headless by goal 7's checks and left running since the morning, 11 h of swiftshader); goal 7's M7 now stops it before chaining into goal 6: 60.0 fps headless. Full check restarted headless.
- D-96 applied: both hosts' CANVAS report lines end with `painted <percent>` (GTK: `painted()` beside `colours()`; web: the driver's canvas dump); goal 6 M2/M6 and goal 8 M5 use it. Headless: goal 6 M2 46% painted, M6 web 48.8 fps playing, goal 8 M5 pass. Full check restarted headless.
- Full headless check: M1–M6, M8–M10 pass; M7 failed on goal 7 M2 ("no Button #0"): with the emulator now stopped after each goal-7 run, every check cold-boots it, and the first app launch takes several seconds more than the driver's fixed 1.5 s wait, so the first tap found the launcher, not the app. The Android driver now waits (up to 60 s) for views of the app's own package before stepping (the launcher behind it has TextViews too). Cold boot: goal 7 M2 and M3 pass. M7 rerun headless.

## Goal 13: keys (2026-10-16 night, owner away)
- Built in a worktree while goal 12's regression ran in the main tree (D-130).
- DataGrid's keyboard passed its own test on the first build; GTK's first build tripped the known "aliasing in String(x[byte=n:])" rule.
- The test step name `type:` was already taken (typing into a field by placeholder): the keyboard one is `typekeys:`.
- Web Escape first did nothing: htmx.ajax with `source: this` merges the input's own hx-vals ({second: 0}) over the values given. Sent from the body now.
- The checker's first M3 failure was the checker's: the follow-on keys replace the status line that proved the write; the Workbook's tap lands on a cell, not a header. Split into two runs, rows per app.
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK): fast typing on the web lost characters (a second key before the editor appeared reopened it: the grid now appends, and the page holds keys typed during the round trip and puts them into the editor in order; the driver types 30 ms apart without waiting); Escape's check couldn't fail (now: Escape then F2 shows the cell's old value, compared with a run that never typed); GTK steps didn't prove focus or the caret (they now say TEST: … when the canvas or editor doesn't have the keyboard, and type at the caret over any selection); every canvas trapped Tab (keys are opt-in: Canvas(keys=True)); GTK sent Ctrl/Alt keys and the web didn't (both send them now); the editor flow was copied in both apps (DataGrid.editor() and cancel_edit() own it); more grid tests (sorted order, edges, exact PageDown/PageUp, Home, scrolling up, before the first draw, fast typing); the comparison filter was put back to goal 12's.
- Review round 2 (NEEDS WORK): "value⏎value⏎" at typing speed lost keys on the web (keys after Enter went into the dying editor). First fix: hold keys page-wide while Enter/Escape are answered and replay them.
- Review round 3 (NEEDS WORK): a held Escape, replayed as a key event, was caught again by the same listener (the page then held every key). Made checks that can fail: `⎋` in typekeys, and a test-only `MOJOUI_EVENT_DELAY_MS` so the web server answers late (a far-away server) for the held-key runs.
- The slow server showed the deeper problem: the typed text lived in two places (the grid on the server, the editor in the browser) and keys raced between them. Root fix (D-132): while a value is typed on the grid, the grid keeps the keyboard and the text; the editor only echoes it (`.echo()`); Enter, Backspace, Escape are the grid's. Then two web bugs the slow runs exposed: each canvas element had its own event queue, so a rebuild dropped the keys still queued (one page-wide queue now); a key pressed while the new screen settled went to a canvas not wired up yet (it's wired first now). "Zed Co⏎AB⏎" against a 250 ms server: both rows written, three runs of three.
- Mistake, second time: `pgrep -f` on the checker's command line matched my own shell and killed it. Encoded: `scripts/stop-check` (by argv and directory).
- Review round 4 (NEEDS WORK): the matrix's "caret after the text" ✅ rested on code nothing ran after D-132 (removed; the row is now the echo editor, D-134, GOAL.md wording changed for the owner to see); GTK rerouted keys from any editor without focus (only echo editors now); Tab/arrows while typing now behave as in Excel (D-135).
- Review round 5: PASS on the code. Small fixes after the full run: the move after a refused write is forgotten on close; the Shift+Tab test starts where it can fail.
- The full run's only failure was goal 7's Android install in the worktree: the emulator held hello signed with the main checkout's debug key (build/android-debug.keystore is per checkout). The worktree uses the main checkout's key; goal 7 M2 passes.

## 2026-10-17 · goal 19: the agent host
- `mojoui/hosts/agent.py`: MCP over stdio by hand (initialize, tools/list, tools/call, ping, list_changed), over the web module. The kit and omxplorer drove on the first run: tools from the tree, a dialog's tools while open, the context menu after a `secondary` gesture, a PNG of a canvas, omxplorer's grid rows via the new `App.describe`.
- Names: the dialog's radio collided with the inspector's segmented (`-2`): now a dialog's or popover's content is prefixed with its title (`settings-done`). Menu paths use the real titles ("in the View › Skin menu").
- The web, in the kit: after File > Save the toast strip sits over the inspector's "Open dialog" button at 1280×800, so a tap there does nothing until it leaves. The check opens the dialog first. Worth a look later: the GTK toast overlay doesn't block.
- The agent host prints nothing itself: stdout is dup'd to stderr at start so the app's own prints (GPU banners) can't corrupt the protocol.
- Found while writing M1's parity step: on the web, a right-click on the canvas while a toast was up opened no context menu. Cause: `place()` runs on htmx:afterSettle, when htmx lends an element whose id is reused (the toast was n66, the new context menu is n66) the old attributes for a moment; the new menu matched `.toast:not([data-timer])` without `data-seconds`, so its timer was `setTimeout(fn, NaN)`: immediate, and it dismissed the menu (its msg2 = on_close). Fix: no timer without seconds, the timer checks it still holds a toast, and dismiss() finds a stale element's successor by kind and title (`data-v` = the shape it was rendered in). The driver now settles after every step as well.

## 2026-10-18 · goal 20: omxplorer on what MX frame can do now
- F-138 reached main at 93f6dbe (fast-forward, by the F-138 session). The package was re-vendored from MX frame main 05332ac (F-140's `scan_parquet`) via `git archive` into `build/mxframe-src`: the MX frame checkout had another session's unfinished F-141 (a conflicted plan.md), so building there would have shipped half-done work (D-186). It built with this repo's Mojo in 11 s and omxplorer linked unchanged.
- Probed the package on pyarrow-written files first: decimal128 stored either as fixed-length bytes or as int64 (`store_decimal_as_integer`), date32, bool, dictionary text with nulls: all read with the right kinds and scales.
- Timings on 100M rows (5 columns, 96 row groups of 2^20, 1.1 GB file): `scan_parquet` opens in 6 ms; a streamed sum takes 4.5 s, a group-by 7.5 s, one row group decodes in 177 ms. So a streamed sheet keeps each column's plain totals (the file doesn't change) and the last 4 decoded groups; a chart costs one group-by, a pivot three.
- `ArcPointer[Pages]` gives the sheet's read-only methods (the grid draws through `self`) a cache they can fill; probed first.
- A row group read by `ParquetScan.read_group` has its text codes mapped to the file's dictionary and its own words cleared: the Import step's samples for text columns go through `scan.text_of`, not the group's table.
- test_streamed's first pivot was across a BOOL column ("groups must be at least 1, got 0"): only text columns group. The test's mistake, not the app's (the inspector only offers text columns).
- The Start screen's copy now names Parquet; goals 14, 15 and 17's checkers looked for the old labels and were updated (D-187). The Workbook and analytics now need the package, so the earlier checkers build their tests with `scripts/mxbuild.sh` (D-189).
- The agent check's peak memory for 100M streamed rows: 0.16 GB, in the agent host's own process (it imports the app module; no subprocess).
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK, 7 items, D-192) and round 2 (D-194). Round 2 caught that adding `grid_formula` to the GridSource trait broke the two test stand-ins (test_datagrid, test_grid_keys): a default body in the trait fixed it with no per-source code. The full check run then in progress was stopped with scripts/stop-check.
- The new deleted-file check failed at first: the read failed (zeros came back) but the status bar didn't say so, because the agent's `read` doesn't rebuild the view and the status bar is built before the grid draws. ## 2026-10-25 · goal 33 M1 and M2: HTTP and timers as effects, JSON decimals
- M1's and M2's proofs written first (scripts/check.py m1, m2; scripts/http_server.py is the test server). M1 checks the server's side too: it saw each request, the env header's value, the POST's content type.
- The owner asked for a generic component (D-320): Transport trait, SocketTransport today, flare later; docs/http.md.
- Dead end: `Pointer(to=x)` handed to C as an out-parameter (getsockopt's SO_ERROR, getaddrinfo's list, pthread_create's id). The compiler kept the old value: a refused connect read as success, then "broken pipe" on send. Fixed with a List buffer C writes into (as the GTK host's `handled`). Worth a line in CLAUDE.md's Mojo rules.
- Dead end: declaring `__errno_location` and `strerror` ourselves clashes with the standard library's own declarations ("existing function with conflicting signature"). Use std.ffi's get_errno() and ErrNo.
- GTK frames stopped while a request was out: the 4 ms effects timeout at the default priority outranks GTK's redraw (120), and the test's pause loop dispatches one priority level per turn. Polls for replies moved to G_PRIORITY_DEFAULT_IDLE; then ticks were 27 ms late, so timers alone run at the default priority (Runtime.timer_wake_in).
- Web: the strip plays only while a reply is out, so the ticks reach the page through /wait, not alongside frames.
- Mac: the stub gains the socket, poll, fcntl, getaddrinfo and two pthread calls; httpcheck builds and links. The AppKit host polls through performSelector:afterDelay:. Not run on a Mac (unverified).
- JSON: Mojo's atof was one ulp off on 5 of 3,000 doubles; strtod_l in the C locale fixed it (D-325). The tool that writes my commands decodes printable \uXXXX escapes, so the corpus file is written with chr(92); check its bytes, not its look.

The only first-run difference was the browser's URL writing ("https://e.com" → "https://e.com/"); safe_url now writes it the same way.
- Two test expectations of mine were wrong, and the original was right: `**a *b* c**` isn't bold (a `*` can't sit inside `**` in its regex), and an unindented line after a list starts a new paragraph. Tests now hold the original's behaviour.
- Tables aren't markdown in the original: the notebook's tables show as one paragraph of `|` text (the recorded shot shows the same). Ours matches.
- Elapsed times: the original's toFixed(1) rounds the double's exact value, so 150 ms reads "0.1s" and 250 ms "0.3s". Matched with fma comparisons. The test's 29 values come from running elapsedText under node. The format has no space: "4.2s", "1m 03s".
- Mojo: my `Span` struct clashed with the standard library's Span (renamed to Run). `@fieldwise_init` can't sit beside an explicit `__init__` that has the same fields. A test's pixel buffer was freed right after `unsafe_ptr()` (its last use), so the Surface read freed memory: keep buffers alive with `_ = len(buf)` after drawing.
- Inline styling: a paragraph with styled runs is laid out as lines of Text pieces (as mojoui.sentence does), broken every ~92 characters at a space. The edges of a piece use no-break spaces so the web doesn't trim "Pipeline:" + " text-generation". A plain paragraph is one Text that the host wraps. For 99 % on the web this break needs measuring, not counting characters (see the M4 worry).

## 2026-10-25 · goal 33 M6 (part b): Add a model
- Message range 7000–7999 is Add's (msgs.mojo); the other M6 screens take their own.
- examples/console/add.mojo (state, the original's pure helpers ported, the screen) and addflow.mojo (each message and reply). The awaits became replies: POST /admin/pulls, GET /admin/pulls/<id> every 0.7 s, load, the models list, the smoke test, the Ready card; a port polls every 1.5 s. Each request carries the run's number (or the search's), so a cancelled run's or an overtaken search's reply is dropped.
- The 250 ms debounce: Every(250, ADD_DEBOUNCE + n % 2). The runtime keeps a timer while its message stays the same, so switching message on each keystroke restarts it.
- console_fixture.py: search answers from the recorded curated list as search.py does (an owner/name query adds a Hugging Face row); a pull moves one step per poll (PULL_SCRIPT); done registers the model, its load makes it ready; ports, cancels and try/chat answer too. The recorded replies are unchanged (M0's shots).
- The load waits 0.8 s and the smoke test 0.3 s in the fixture. Without that every stage after Fetch took 0.0s, and M6 wants 4 different elapsed times (its texts() is a set).
- New in mojoui: catalog.mojo (OutlinedField, FieldWithHelp, FilterChips, ResultRow, FitMark, Meter, EmptyState, BackButton; catalog_roles), logview.LogPane (head, Hide log, coloured lines; logpane_roles), http.uri_component, clock.clock_time, PageHeader(hue=, lead=), IconSquare(hue=), 3 Lucide icons (triangle-alert, hammer, refresh-cw). ## Goal 36b: zero-copy presentation (2026-10-28)
- Desktop facts (probed): Hyprland 0.56.2, one monitor HDMI-A-2 3440×1440 @ 59.97 Hz, scale 1.6, VRR off, tearing off; DPMS reads off (owner away) yet GTK's frame clock still ticks (a scripted C run finished). GTK 4.22.4 picks GskVulkanRenderer on Wayland. Two RTX 3090s: CUDA 0 = 2F:00.0 (renderD128), CUDA 1 = 30:00.0 (renderD129, the compositor's main device; cuGLGetDevices on GDK's GL context says CUDA 1). Mojo's DeviceContext(device_id=1) uses that device's primary context (cuCtxGetCurrent == cuDevicePrimaryCtxRetain(1)).
- Scale: on Wayland GTK draws the 1280×720-point canvas at 2048×1152 pixels (GDK_SCALE ignored); the browsers' canvases stay 1280×720 pixels and are scaled up. Goal 36's C desktop baseline as-is: 10.1 ms/frame at 1M (Vulkan renderer), 8.9 ms with GSK_RENDERER=gl, both 2048×1152.
- Approach 1, CUDA → dmabuf directly: CU_DEVICE_ATTRIBUTE_DMA_BUF_SUPPORTED = 0 on both GPUs, cuMemGetHandleForAddressRange → 801 (not supported). Dead end on this driver (610.57.04, proprietary modules).
- Approach 2, CUDA → GL texture (cuGraphicsGLRegisterImage) → GdkGLTexture, GTK's default Vulkan renderer: works, but GTK exports the GL texture to a dmabuf to import it into Vulkan and NVIDIA's EGL refuses ("Creating EGLImage for dmabuf failed: 0x3002", with or without the CUDA registration, glTexImage2D or glTexStorage2D), so GTK downloads it: 3.4 ms/frame in a Python probe, no better than a memory texture (3.9).
- Approach 2 with GTK's GL renderer (GSK_RENDERER=gl): the texture is used as it is (same share group): 0.58 ms/frame in the probe (vs 2.2 for a memory texture on the same renderer). Orientation: CUDA row 0 lands on the picture's top row (both renderers).
- Approach 3, GBM dmabuf (renderD128, NVIDIA modifier 0x300000000606014) → EGLImage → cuGraphicsEGLRegisterImage → GdkDmabufTexture on the Vulkan renderer: works on GPU 0 ("Vulkan uploaded 1280x720 AB24 dmabuf", 1.7 ms/frame in the probe); on GPU 1 GTK aborts (gsk_vulkan_device_find_allocator assertion: GTK's Vulkan device 0 is the other GPU); the GL-preferred modifier 0x300000000e08014 is refused by Vulkan (downloaded through GL); LINEAR crashed in gbm. An abort can't be a run-time fallback, so it isn't the default.
- Built (05:20–05:50): mojoui/present.mojo (Presenter: CUDA↔GL texture ring, the painter, a windowless EGL context for tests), Surface's GPU fields, PointCloud handing pictures over / painting / the packed splat, the GTK host's probe (before the app exists, so Gpu() picks the display's GPU), GdkGLTextureBuilder hand-over with a release callback. tests/test_canvas_present.mojo passed first run (byte for byte at 1280×720 and 333×197); test_canvas_blend: additive points equal the compute splat exactly (0 of 921,600 pixels differ).
- Desktop, 1280×720 px, vsync off, scripted: 1M frame 5.43 ms (readback) → 1.18 (GPU→GPU copy) → 1.14 (blended). 5M: 3 atomics 1.71, packed 1.51, blended 2.31. 20M: 3.97 / 3.19 / 7.70. Blending loses once the points pile up (no saturation skip in fixed-function blending: the 20M blend frame grows from 6 to 14.7 ms as the ring forms, the splat's falls from 11 to 2.7 ms).
- First auto-choice tried alternating frames: wrong (switching CUDA ↔ GL work every frame cost both; the splat read 7.5 ms). Blocks of frames fixed that, but the choice made in the first 20 frames held for 600 frames while the work changed (20M mean 7.2 ms); a drift trigger on the chosen way's cost reacted too late (blending's GL time only shows in the next frame's wait). Now: re-measure after 60 frames, doubling while it holds, a tenth's margin to switch: 20M 3.43 ms (vs 3.19 splat-only: the re-measurements cost ~0.2 ms a frame), 1M 1.28 (vs 1.18).
- Pipelining tried (06:00–06:50): (1) no CPU wait at all, the copy queued after the kernels on stream 0: correct (the M6 test's 2M-point/20-queued-steps case) but every frame 4.67 ms: GL stalls while CUDA work is queued; (2) a wait after the copy: back to 1.25; (3) one frame in flight: 1M 0.89–0.96 ms, but 5M/20M 4.4–4.6 (GTK's GL drawing of the last frame runs into this frame's CUDA work); keeping the texture mapped until its copy finished didn't change that, so the stall isn't the unmap but GL and CUDA sharing the GPU; (4) a measured on/off choice per canvas: confounded by the blending choice's own measuring, chose wrongly on early frames. Kept: waits before GTK draws (D-746), one in flight opt-in (D-747).
- Native Rust in a Wayland window (wgpu, Vulkan): 18.5 ms/frame at 1M and 20M with Immediate or Mailbox (both offered): paced by the compositor (GTK with its frame clock capped reads 18.8 ms here too). Its throughput is only measurable offscreen; the Mailbox experiment was reverted (B unchanged).
- First full desktop run (commit 3a2b8d3, 64 min, all gated; not kept: a caveat in it was false). Frame ms 1M/5M/20M: A 1.23/1.34/3.01, B-native offscreen 0.34/1.52/5.98, B-native in a window 16.65 at every N (60 Hz: the earlier 18.5 ms probe was a slower moment of the compositor; the caveat said 18.5), B-wasm 1.88/16.67/53.03, C 1.23/1.61/3.25. Features: goal 36's path on the desktop 5.61/6.19/8.73 (so the Xvfb software renderer was not the cost), +M6 1.24/1.73/3.79, +M6c 1.22/1.55/3.05, +M6b measured 1.23/1.61/3.25 (its trials cost 0.06–0.2 ms), blending always 1.22/6.16/11.22, C at 2048×1152 1.34/1.79/3.75.
- Fixed before the final run: blending's trials measure the splat first and stop blending once a frame of it costs 1.5× the splat (spot check 5M 1.54, 20M 3.08); the window caveat no longer quotes 18.5 ms; B-native's "(in a window)" label only on the desktop report.
- Not done, on purpose: fusing step and splat into one pass (saves reading every point twice; would likely put C ahead of A at 5M+). A and B also run two passes, and tuning C after seeing A's desktop numbers without giving A the same chance is out of bounds (GOAL.md fair play). Next lever, for all three stacks alike.
- Review (NEEDS WORK) fixes: draw_vertices tested a List (always true) instead of vbo_mapped — an empty draw gave up blending (new failing test, then fixed); App.GPU_CANVAS gate with tests/test_gpu_canvas_gate.mojo and an end-to-end check that hello isn't probed; close()/release_cuda with a test (and "why 'closed'" seen at a desktop shutdown); copy_in/give_up never leave anything mapped; one-in-flight pending picture freed or shown when the canvas stops. Probe of the confound (one run each, 1M): goal 36's path 5.66 ms on Vulkan, 4.44 on GL, the picture kept on the GPU 1.24: about 1.2 ms was the renderer, 3.2 the readback and CPU hand-over.
- The lever not pulled (fair play): fusing step and draw into one pass is the next lever for every stack; not tuned for omxcode alone.

## Goal 38 fair play, build D (2026-10-28): what the fairness reviewer found
- Before (engine Query full scan each change, CPU cents, no sort): 100M median input-to-pixels 37.4 ms, p95 74.6; GPU compute median 24.7 ms; load read 1.66 s + cents 0.38 s + upload 1.38 s; cold start 3.7 s.
- Engine checked first: `Query` has no row range (the executor slices rows internally for jobs, `_table_addresses(row0, rows)`, not public); `range_aggregate` is CPU, ungrouped, unfiltered; DeviceTable is int64 throughout. So: mojoui.tableprep (sort at load, cents on every core), mojoui.devgroup (GPU group-by over a row slice), devrows over slices with reused buffers (requests #24, #25; #22 unchanged).
- Sort at load: first 2.4 s at 100M; zero-filled 800 MB buffers were 0.9 s of it (`List(length=n, fill=0)` single-threaded) → `unsafe_uninit_length`: 1.3 s. 24 logical vs 12 physical cores: no change. GPU sort not done (host copy must match, D-778).
- Group-by over the slice, first kernel: filtered all-rows 25.9 ms, worse than unfiltered (13 ms). Staging the program in shared memory: 25.9 → ~18. Rows sorted by day put a warp's 32 lanes on one day slot: warp-uniform add (vote + shuffles, lane 0 adds once) → 12.1. Per-warp copies of the shared accumulators: no change (reverted).
- Three groupings measured in the app (D-780): product × day one pass won every step; kept.
- After (one run, GPU shared with 8 mxframe test processes, provisional): 100M median input-to-pixels 19.7 ms, worst 64.0; GPU compute median 3.0 ms; load read 1.4 + cents 0.16 + sort 1.26 + upload 1.5 s; cold start 4.5 s (the sort costs ~0.8 s of cold start net). check_d: D ok at 10M and 100M. The median change-to-frame is now mostly the job being polled once a display frame, not compute.
- External drive (D-770): `App.input_lines` (any app; GTK host watches stdin on its main loop; `read` looked up with dlsym since the standard library declares it its own way) and the dashboard's `--scripted-external`. Proved headless: 32 steps over stdin at 10M and 100M, `reference.compare` clean; a bad line refused; stdin closed mid-step still records that step before exiting. check_d re-run after: D ok (10M), D ok (100M) (load average 16 from other agents during it: read 3.0 s that run, so this run's timings aren't comparable).
- Driver contract (docs/goals/38/README.md, committed by the harness meanwhile): D now prints `ready <json>` (load figures), `shown N <ms> {"gpu_ms", "compute_ms"}`, `error <text>`, `bye`, and takes `step 0`. Proved headless at 10M (opening, step 0 re-applied, 32 steps: 33 `shown`, `bye`, compare clean). check_d after: D ok (10M), D ok (100M): 100M median change-to-frame 19.9 ms, GPU compute median 3.4 ms, load 1.5 + 0.17 + 1.37 + 1.3 s, cold start 4.6 s.
- Job wake (D-771): examples/jobwake (a canvas animating, 40 jobs of 2–9 ms, job end → update() timed in the app). GTK headless: wake median 0.05 ms, p95 1.0–2.2 ms, worst 2.2–2.9 ms (the tail: a job ending while the main thread paints a frame; update() can't pre-empt it) vs frame poll only: median 10.9 ms, worst 15 ms. Web host, no browser attached (0 frame ticks, so the wake alone drives it): median 0.08 ms, worst 0.28 ms. Mac: cross-builds and links with no new C symbols; not run (Mac testing needs GitHub's runners = a push). UIKit not wired.
- Dashboard A/B on one binary (MOJOUI_JOB_WAKE=0 / 1, interleaved, load average ~22 from other agents, provisional): 100M median input-to-pixels 23.1, 24.9 → 19.7, 19.2 ms; 10M 25.9, 25.2 → 18.7, 18.8 ms. What's left is mostly the wait for the next display frame (60 Hz headless) to draw. check_d: D ok (10M), D ok (100M).
- Goal 30: M2 PASS. M1 failed twice, both times on its own 3.5 s budget for opening the 100M file (the open landed at 3.6 s and 4.4 s under load averages 40 and 24); omxplorer doesn't opt into jobs_message nor use start_ui_job, so this change isn't in that path. Needs a rerun on a quiet machine before it counts.
- Fairness round 2: kpi_rect/results_rect in `ready` (mojoui.geometry, GTK lookup), checked by drawing both on a screenshot (they frame the KPI row and the chart+table exactly); README states integer cents and the per-day-histogram cut-off as D's shortcuts. check_d: D ok (10M), D ok (100M).

Not ImageOps: the test made `Surface(Int(px.unsafe_ptr()), …)` and never used `px` again, so Mojo freed the pixels at once and every draw wrote into freed memory. `_ = len(px)` after the last draw fixed it. Rule: a List lent to a Surface by address must be used after the last draw.

- Prototyped in Python first (`/tmp`, not kept). HF image-guided scores through a sigmoid were all 1.0 → raw logits (D-963). ## 2026-10-30 · goal 40 (Sketch) set up
- Why: the Personal Note audit (personalnotev2/docs/research/omxcode-gap-audit.md) found drawing is the biggest untested family: Scene has no paths, events no pressure, the Mac canvas is CPU pixels.

One Font per Screens, previews rebuilt when a component finishes or 64 bytes arrive: 76,700 events/s (2,004 events in 26 ms), the whole chat's view in 86 µs.
- M5 (b): first version put MX frame inside genui; every genui user then had to link the engine's Parquet codecs (test_genui and goal 35's checks stopped linking). Pivot (D-4744): mojoui.datahub (Hub trait, NoData default) + mojoui.sheethub (SheetHub over MX frame sheets); GenUi/Screens/AgentChat generic over it. DataGrid/ChartView take dataset (+ group_by, value, combine, top); the chart's numbers are Sheet.totals; a caption says the cost ("1,000,000 rows · 0.3 ms on the GPU"); the agent hears of the datasets in each run's context. test_local_agent: 1M rows, chart equal to a plain loop. agentchat holds a million generated orders (AGENTCHAT_ORDERS) and an "orders" scenario charts them: build/g48-orders.png.

- M6: goal 35's M2/M3 read the old app's composition and status line: the example offers render_ui itself, AgentChat's developer mode shows "streamed: N pieces" (D-4745); matrix rows for AgentChat, Rows, SheetHub, Arrive on a spring; SF Symbols for the new icons. scripts/check: M0–M6 PASS.
- Reviewer round 1: NEEDS WORK. Fixed: agentchat cut() at a character's start (a crash on non-ASCII agent text past 137/87/500 bytes; test with accents and Japanese at every cut); partialjson stops past 64 levels (16,000 '[' took 175 ms: quadratic copies; hostile prefixes now in test_agui_stream, < 200 ms); Screens.unfinished on the stream's end (a stopped render_ui said "Building UI" forever; test "unfinished: ok"); the web replayed .arrive() on every swap (86 animations for 2 views; the morph reset the class): a MutationObserver marks views already seen before the next paint (2 animations); C_CODE/C_COPY ranges bounded; sheethub's top-N a selection (no groups² sort); test_genui refuses a wrong-typed prop for each component (34); agui_bench.py writes docs/agui.md's Benchmark section from bench.json with losses computed (the docs had an older run's numbers, and hid that CopilotKit's first UI beat the web host's in that run); M5 checks the section matches bench.json (D-4746). Not done: per-node group-by cache in previews, keeping the last good preview on a half-written group_by, MAX_UIS never freed (40 screens a session), moving sampledata out of mojoui.
- Under load (load average 16: other worktrees' checks), goal 35's M0 web trickle counted 56 frames in 2 s (< 60) once; the re-run passed. A flaky threshold under a loaded machine, not a change here.
- Owner: "can we fix web dropping frames". Attacked the premise first: the planned fix (patch only changed views) assumed DOM swaps were the cost, but the page held 233 elements. A Chromium timeline trace of the paced stream (load average ~20): 15,156 timer callbacks, 4.9 s of a 10 s run; 2,722 calls to the page's whole() (2.8 s: each walked every style sheet's rules, then set and read a width per pill, 8,852 forced layouts), five hooks queued per out-of-band element. afterSwaps(fn) runs each hook once per answer; whole() caches its selectors and measures in one layout. Same machine, same load: 104 rAF gaps over 25 ms → 12 (worst 116 → 33 ms), and the page took 714 answers instead of 231. agui_bench.py (web + CopilotKit, median of 3): paced web 0 dropped frames (worst 16.8 ms), burst 0; CopilotKit 0 paced, 3 in the burst (worst 200 ms). 48-check M5 now refuses a per-swap timer in web.py (D-4747).
- Owner: a gallery, the cover, and a page that walks through AG-UI. docs/agents.html (gallery, what it does, how it works, use it, what you need, speed, where, not yet), checked in headless Chromium at 1440 and 390 px; omxqua's own `.side`/`.grp` class names clashed with the page's, renamed. Cover #agents rewritten around the Lisbon map; gallery tile → the dashboard; docs/gallery gains an Agent chat card; component counts regenerated (124). Not pushed: the site publishes from main and goal 48 isn't merged (D-4748).
- Owner: no speed on the public pages, "compliant and in process" instead (D-4749). agents.html's Speed section replaced; the hero, cover lede, cover pills, gallery tile and gallery card lose every timing and CopilotKit mention (grep: none left); checked in headless Chromium.
- Owner: start the posts simply, with a getting-started page. examples/start (a counter in Liquid) built on GTK (~70 s first build) and the web, tapped three times by the test mode on both (shots); the same file without theme() shot for the comparison (a throwaway example, deleted); the agent host read its screen and tapped it ("Now: Tapped 1 times"). docs/start.html checked at 1440 and 390 px (a single-column grid overflowed on phones: minmax(0, 1fr)). Every app build prints ~23 deprecation warnings from the framework's own code (unsafe_ptr → ptr, positional __getitem__): the page says so; cleaning them is open. Posts drafted in docs/launch/series.md (D-4750).
- Mac: examples/start built for the Mac (cross, 48 s) and run on GitHub's macOS 15 runner: taps work, layout wrong (the root VStack is 121 pt wide in a 480 pt window; on GTK and the web it fills the window). A Mac host bug to fix next; the page says so (D-4751). Also a 60-second interactive film for the posts (docs/launch/series/film.html).
- Film, AG-UI part: recording real frames of the Lisbon answer showed the web screen stuck on its loading card for 4.4 s, then jumping in, once flashing back. Not the web host: partial_ui returned the loading card for 20 of 24 deltas (a probe over each prefix); complete() put the Skeleton right after an unfinished child with no comma. The existing test only checked the validator accepted each cut, and LOADING_UI is valid, so it passed. Fixed and tested (D-4752). Then the film's ending rebuilt from real frames and events; the reviewer caught a hand-written "tool result" line and unrecorded second-run times: both now recorded (D-4753).
- AG-UI compatibility README for the owner's posts. Before writing it, the one unproven direction was closed: Pydantic AI's AGUIAdapter (pydantic-ai-slim 2.66.0, same ag-ui-protocol 1.0.0) served a FunctionModel; the Mojo app read its stream, drew its render_ui screen and answered the call on GTK and the web (scripts/agui_interop.py). A meter on the demo screen read "Events read: all of them": nothing counted that, removed. Also noticed in today's bench: with the partialjson fix, streaming previews really rebuild, and the web host's paced run shows 8 frame gaps over 25 ms (worst 33 ms); the earlier "0" was measured while previews were stuck on the loading card (D-4752). docs/agui.md's benchmark text says so; next: send only changed views on the web.
- AG-UI reasoning and chunks (D-4757, D-4758). Reading @ag-ui/client's transformChunks first showed two real bugs in our CHUNK handling: later chunks may omit the id (we refused them), and a chunked tool call ends when the next event arrives (we never marked it done, so render_ui as chunks would never draw). tests/test_agui_chunks.mojo covers text, tool and reasoning chunks plus the chat (Thinking… → Thought, unfold, a chunked screen drawn); it's in 48-check M1. Interop on four runs (Pydantic AI with thinking, LangGraph; GTK and the web) all PASS. Two false failures on the way, both from the check: a stale agentchat build (g5() doesn't rebuild), and LangGraph's in-memory checkpointer carrying the GTK conversation into the web run (fixed with a reset per host). Pydantic's scripted model first put thinking and the tool call on the same part index (its error came back as RUN_ERROR, shown by the chat), fixed with index 1.
- Agent catalog: Picture (by name, from the app's Album) and Disclosure (D-4759). test_genui renders and refuses both with the rest (catalog: ok 38), plus picture (an unknown name, an app with none, a link refused; the caption; the pixels drawn) and disclosure (folded, opened, folded again; open: true). 48-check M4's trip now needs the picture's caption and the opened "Getting around" list on screen, on GTK and the web: PASS. The illustration is ours (PIL), so the repo carries no third-party photo.
- agentchat on the Mac (D-4760, run 41994306002): it builds for AppKit after adding nine libSystem calls to the stub, and runs the replayed Lisbon trip on macOS 15 and 26. AG-UI works there: the requests validate, the screen and Disclosure work, and the tap goes back. Layout doesn't: once the trip arrives, its map canvas is 808 pt in a 715 pt column and the window's content shrinks to 435 pt, so the trip spills off the right (docs/img/agents/mac-trip-overflow.png). Canvases with .fill() don't give up width on AppKit as they do on GTK and the web. That's the next Mac fix (the host, not AG-UI); the runners have no ffmpeg, so the Picture path is unrun there.
- Tutorial 2, a todo app (D-4761). examples/tasks runs the same on GTK and the web (CHECK: Buy milk=on, Call Ada=off, 1 left) and keeps its tasks across a restart (OMX_CONFIG in a temp folder). The page's code blocks are cut from the file by line range, and scripts/tutorials.py fails when a block drifts (tried: an edited bind line → "a block isn't examples/tasks/app.mojo's text"). The agent step caught two things: every row's button was "Remove" (the agent got remove, remove-2 with no hint; a tag became a tool name with spaces, so a tooltip it is), and a checkbox tool called without "on" cleared the box and said "Did" (the host never checked required arguments; it does now).
- The learning deck (D-4762). First screenshots were unreadable: reveal.js paints .reveal-viewport white, and omxqua's tokens override a plain :root --ink with a more specific selector, so the deck has its own --d-* colours and sets --r-background-color. Long code lines are clipped (overflow hidden at 1280×720), so code panes take the wider column, long blocks use the small size, and the LocalAgent docstring wraps. The agent transcript on the slides had to follow a real order (add both, then the screen; refuse, then tick), so the check now keeps the screen from before the tick too and reads every output block on a page, not only the first.
- The deck's two ideas first (D-4764; reviewer round 1: a pixel function is called on the CPU, not an automatic fallback; the example's picture is an Image, not a tool; no Apple-GPU claim; AMD untested; no "fast" in notes). isoarch floors overlap at deck size: with three floors on one footprint the upper plate hides the middle one unless the gap is so large the drawing shrinks to nothing, so the main diagram is two floors (Mojo / already there) with zones for app.mojo and omxcode. Inside the deck the diagrams are iframes; file:// iframes are separate origins in Chromium, so checking needs a local http server (GitHub Pages is one origin, fine). The skill's template has no reset call; dispatching "change" on its flow picker resets a flow. Fact-finding for the GPU slides corrected two things the owner's brief assumed: the best GPU apps do draw with the GPU (Zed, Blender, Figma, Chrome, and GTK 4 and Core Animation composite on it), and omxcode's own GPU path copies the picture back to the CPU unless an app opts into the CUDA–GL path (examples/particles only). Both are said on the slides.
- The deck in the owner's order (D-4765). focus() gains data-pairs so one step lights lines in two blocks; reveal's current-visible fragments make a caption bar that shows one sentence at a time, which fits a two-column code comparison where a list of sentences didn't.

## Goal 50 (2026-11-04): Flare, HTTPS, serve, process, omxconsole's daemon
- Flare v0.12.0 runs on Mojo 1.1 (pixi.toml at the tag: mojo >=1.1,<2); v0.13 needs 1.2. So no Mojo bump (D-4779).
- Vendored flare + json from the GitHub tarballs (gh api …/tarball/v0.12.0). Shims compile with Flare's own lines against the pixi env (OpenSSL, zlib, brotli libs; brotli_wrapper.c needs no headers). rustls/QUIC skipped.
- Flare alone: builds in 38 s, a refused port is a clean NetworkError.
- mojoui + Flare: "existing function with conflicting signature" on inet_pton, then pthread_create (Flare's entry is `def(Pointer[UInt8]) thin -> Pointer[UInt8]`, pointee types don't matter, Int vs pointer does). 27 shared symbols → mojoui/libc.mojo with Flare's types + scripts/libc_lint.py.
- `Pointer(unsafe_from_address=0)` with a literal trips "Pointer is non-nullable" at compile time; through a variable it's fine (Flare's _null_ptr trick).
- Regression: omxplorer failed on strtod (the engine's csv.mojo declares (Int, Int)); the engine also declares pthread_create/pthread_detach its own way. Calling those three through dlsym fixes both sides (D-4786).
- test_http had to be started with HTTP_TEST_URL/DEAD/SECRET (my check launched the server bare): fixed the check, not the proof (D-4784).
- HTTPS: Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct's config.json, 659 bytes = the HF file list's size, through effects on a worker.
- mojoui.serve, first run: the server listened but never accepted (Recv-Q 11): the binary ran outside pixi and Flare's dlopen of libflare_tls.so failed in every worker (only visible with one worker, which raises in main). RUNPATH fix (D-4767).
- Handler calls with user agent "node" on any port I open: another program on this machine probes ports; not Flare.
- `spawn[f: def(Int) -> None]` refuses a plain def ("a thin function cannot bind to a closure trait"): parameter type `def(Int) thin -> None`.
- `Atomic.fetch_sub` isn't static; mojoui uses `_A.fetch_add(ptr, -1)`.
- test_serve: 50 parallel read-modify-writes all counted (the lock); 6 SSE events over 301 ms with a request answered meanwhile in 6 ms; the proxy relays them at the same pace; a dead upstream is a 502.
- M2: the OpenAI Python SDK streamed 12 pieces from tests/serve_demo.mojo.
- Note: this worktree's .pixi was copied (reflink) from mojo-ui-omxcode's; the Mojo it runs still points at that env's lib paths (RUNPATH shows them). Same Mojo 1.1; new packages (openai) went into this copy only.
- mojoui.process: `posix_spawnp(…, cargs[0], …, Int(cargs.unsafe_ptr()), …)` in one call: "use of invalidated interior reference" (an element and the list's pointer in one call); take them into locals first.
- Counting processes through /proc/*/cmdline from Mojo found 1 of 3 (the files report size 0; read() then reads little); `kill(-pgid, 0)` asks the group directly.
- test_process: SIGTERM'd child code 143; serve_demo healthy in 250 ms; crash exit 3 with its log tail; a TERM-ignoring group gone 622 ms after stop(600) with SIGKILL (137); a dropped Process stops its child.
- consoled (M4): a helper agent wrote docs/goals/50/admin-api-spec.md from the Python daemon (389 lines, errors checked against the running app); the store's replies are built by SQLite (json_object / json_group_array), the rest in views.mojo.
- `cp mst.db` misses the WAL (a copy without the labels table); scripts/store_copy.py and the check use SQLite's backup API.
- An error inside a transaction left it open ("cannot start a transaction within a transaction"): answer() rolls back on any error.
- A parameter named `range` shadows the builtin (`'String' does not implement __call__`).
- M4's first run: only metrics by_model / series models differed (keyed by model name; the copied store has no requests in 5 minutes): the check compares them as maps now, M5 requires the chatted model in both (D-4773).
- M4 PASS: 18 GETs in the recorded shapes, 13 models, labels and hashed keys across a restart, console Models / Overview / Keys on GTK against consoled (docs/goals/50/console-*-consoled-gtk.png).
- M5, try 1: `max serve` from this repo's env fails (MAX without its serving extras: no opentelemetry exporter). consoled runs mxstudio's install through MXC_MAX (D-4774).
- Try 2: "failed to resolve built-in kernel package paths": the child inherited this env's CONDA_PREFIX; try 3 still failed until MODULAR_HOME=<env>/share/max (set by that env's activation) was passed too. consoled now gives `max serve` its own env's variables. Model up in 8.6 s.
- Try 4: max serve 400 "Missing JSON": Flare 0.12's send_streaming writes only the request head (raw bytes captured on a socket). Patched the vendored copy (D-4775), upstream report drafted.
- Try 5: everything but the idle unload: a child still starting at the chat was never probed again, so never "ready", so never idle. The sweep probes starting children.
- M5 PASS: Qwen2.5-0.5B streamed 9 pieces through consoled's /v1 with the OpenAI SDK; 401 without a key; the request in /admin/requests and metrics by_model; max serve gone after the 45 s idle.
- First full run: M0–M5 PASS, M6 FAIL: max serve's own Python workers listed as "Python under consoled" (exempted by argv match on `max serve` and its descendants, D-4776), and goal 33's M5 (console vs fixture: stray `GET /`, faster polls). Baseline at bfacc58 (../mojo-ui-g50-baseline) fails goal 33's M5 the same way: not this goal's regression.
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK (M6 writeup, the substring exemption, bad %XX escapes, no SIGTERM handling, slow work under the lock, a write per /v1 call, pipe leaks). Fixed (D-4777). Signal blocking in main didn't help (Mojo's runtime threads exist before main: exit 143); `var` at module scope: "global variables are not supported"; the self-pipe trick (send() to a fixed fd from the handler) works: serve_demo exits 0 on SIGTERM, consoled's max serve goes with it.
- test_serve's proxy relayed 0 events in 3 of 8 runs after the changes: it proxied to its own server, whose worker was waiting for that very request's head. A second upstream server: 10 of 10.
- The tightened exemption first missed `max serve` itself: MAX's bin/max execs `python -c "...from max._entrypoints.pipelines import main..." serve`, so no argv is named max. The check matches that entry point too.
- Found two orphaned `max serve` (Qwen, ~1 GB GPU each) from the two M5 runs stopped at 23:46 and 23:51 (before consoled handled SIGTERM; their MXC_HOME was build/g50/mxc). Stopped their groups. With D-4777 a stopped consoled takes its models with it (checked by hand: load, SIGTERM, none left).
- Final: M0–M5 PASS; M6 FAIL only on goal 33's M5 (same as unchanged main).

## Goal 52 (2026-11-05): Mojo 1.2, Flare 0.13, the engine beside Flare
Owner: "you can move to Mojo 1.2 .. not a big change anyway". Worktree ../mojo-ui-g52 (branch goal-52-mojo12 from
goal-50-native 4352a74), its own env (D-5201). The 2026-10-26 probe (branch probe-mojo12, docs/research/mojo12-flare.md)
was the map.
- Env: `pixi install` with `mojo >=1.2,<1.3` → Mojo 1.2.0 (2016d0cd), MAX 26.7.0; ../mojo-ui and ../mojo-ui-g50 still 1.1.
- Baseline: scripts/sweep.py over ../mojo-ui-g50 on 1.1: 96/101 example builds, 100/137 tests (the rest need data in
  build/g39, g41, g47, a server, torch or a display bus; dictate and omxplorer's Mac link already fail there).
- Engine: 834ef9a's archive builds on 1.2 after the accelerator query swap (6 lines; probe of `default_accelerator()` on
  Linux/Mac/Mac+metal/iOS gives the same answers as 1.1's call). Its test runner said every test "uses Python": the ldd
  grep matches PyPI's library path (D-5209); with that narrowed, 40/40 engine tests pass.
- Flare 0.13.1 + json 0.4.2 vendored; the 0.12 stream-body patch applies with offsets (0.13.1 still drops the body);
  flare#42 (CA bundle) is fixed upstream. test_flare_link built first try.
- test_https/test_serve/test_process failed bare: they need goal 50's checker's set-up (build/g50, serve_demo, the TLS
  server). A dlsym-with-CStringSpan suspicion was checked and wrong (probe: same addresses on 1.1 and 1.2).
- test_serve hung in goal 50's M2 on 1.2. Two causes, both Flare 0.13's new runtime: (1) its multi-worker serve closes
  the bound listener and every worker binds its own, so a connect right after Server.start was refused; (2) its serving
  loops run as tasks on Mojo's runtime pool, and the runtime waits for pool tasks at exit (gdb: main in
  KGEN_CompilerRT_DestroyGlobals → ReleaseCPUDevice). Server.start now returns a `Served` and waits for a worker's answer
  (D-5206). First version stopped at once: Mojo drops a value after its last use (`var port = server.port`).
- Engine + Flare in one program (M5): the engine's libc declarations moved behind dlsym (D-5204); then an engine job
  inside a request hung: the job's `parallelize` waited in AsyncRT synchronize while Flare's serving loops held pool
  workers (it ran fine before the server started). mojoui.serve now holds Flare's pool leases so its loops go to OS
  threads (D-5207): 8 concurrent requests, each an engine group-by, all correct.
- M2's first run: (1) `patch` left vendor/flare/http/client.mojo.orig (the tree diff caught it); (2) test_https got a
  200 with a body that wasn't JSON: 0.13's client asks for gzip and its zlib shim, built by our script without
  -ldeflate, never loaded ("undefined symbol: libdeflate_gzip_compress"), so the body stayed compressed (D-5211);
  (3) goal 50's M0 pins v0.12.0 by name (D-5212). Then PASS: the tag alone sends a streamed POST with no body, the patch
  sends it.
- M3 sweep 1 (old objc line): every Mac/iPhone build failed on `String(unsafe_from_utf8_ptr=Pointer[Int8])` (the probe's
  one Mac line; fixed to UInt8). Elsewhere nothing lost but test_process, which starts build/serve_demo (the 1.1
  checkout had one from goal 50's checker; M3 now builds it first).
- omxplorer's Mac cross-link fails on 1.1 too (baseline): its video, document and vision parts need Python and libc
  functions apple/sdk's stubs don't list. Not this goal's; M6 is left as written.
- M7 (owner mid-run: "ensure it does not interfere with our UI thread and blocks"): mojoui's HTTP runs on effects' own
  threads (the UI only polls ready()), so the open questions were mojoui.serve's start/stop and shared locks. A frame
  loop on the main thread (4 ms ticks) through 27 requests at once (SSE, proxy, engine jobs, HTTPS): longest gap 4-5 ms.
  But stop() held the caller ~100 ms per server (waiting for Flare's 50 ms loop): now it only asks, and the record is
  shared and freed by the last owner (D-5214; a reaper-thread version could read freed memory, dropped). Server.start
  once took 515 ms: its ready probe hit the listener Flare was about to close and waited out a 500 ms timeout; 50 ms
  probes: ≤ 28 ms over 15 runs. A first 502 was the test's own race (proxies before PUT /port).
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK (no memory-safety bug in the Served/_Run refcount). The sharpest find was mine while
  fixing it: patch_scope matched only `+++ w/`/`b/` lines and both patches carried git's mnemonic `c/`/`i/` prefixes,
  so its file check had passed on an empty set; fixed, regenerated with a/ b/, and negative-tested with doctored
  patches. Fixes in D-5216. Macs: macOS 26's first job never got a runner (cancelled after 15 min queued); its rerun
  passed (maccheck exit 0, 110 side pictures, console renders).
- Full run after review fixes: M1 failed: engine test_switches_threads segfaulted (30/30 once rebuilt) in pthread_join of
  thread "0". Cause: `pthread_create(Int(Pointer(to=tid)), ...)` through my dlsym wrapper: the local's address as an
  Int doesn't tell the compiler the call writes `tid`. Fixed in the wrapper (returns the id from its own cell) and in
  framefeed (same line, mine); a lint now rejects the pattern (D-5217). 0/30 failures after.

## 2026-11-05 · D-5310: GPU pictures stay on the GPU (Linux)
- `Gpu.draw` added; test first (tests/test_gpu_draw.mojo failed to parse without it, then PASS: CPU = GPU copy = GL texture, 333×197 and 1920×1080, the surface's main memory never written on the GPU path).
- examples/gpu now draws a Canvas. On the desktop (WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-1, MOJOUI_PRESENT_STATS=1): "GPU canvas pictures go to the screen without a copy to the CPU … CUDA device 1 → GL texture on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090", gpu_copied 3, cpu_copied 0. Headless (Xvfb, cairo): falls back to the copy, the CPU check still ✓; CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="": drawn on the CPU, no CRITICAL.
- lens declares GPU_CANVAS: on the desktop gpu_copied 1, cpu_copied 0.
- The live deck's GPU slides and diagram now say Linux + NVIDIA stays on the GPU, the Mac and the web copy for now.
- Gotcha: my shell had no WAYLAND_DISPLAY; GTK then crashes in gtk_icon_theme (no display). Set WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-1 for desktop runs.
- Review round 1 (NEEDS WORK): the test now compares 1920×1080 in full, goes back down to 333×197 in the same buffer, and skips a 0×0 canvas (Gpu.draw returns early). The reviewer's desktop run saw gpu_copied 0: test mode only shows the window with MOJOUI_SHOT, so nothing draws without it. The proof command: `env -u DISPLAY -u GSK_RENDERER WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-1 MOJOUI_HEADLESS=0 GDK_BACKEND=wayland MOJOUI_PRESENT_STATS=1 MOJOUI_TEST="slide:0=40;slide:0=60" MOJOUI_SHOT=/tmp/shot.png build/gpu/linux/gpu` → gpu_copied 3, cpu_copied 0, three runs out of three.

## 2026-11-05 · D-5311: the Mac's GPU picture in an IOSurface
- MAX 26.7 programs Apple silicon GPUs (M1–M5) through Metal (max.modular.com/stable/gpu/hardware-compatibility); `wrap_host_memory` lends host memory to the GPU (Metal: page-aligned, whole pages). The native Mac GPU build already existed for omxplorer (omxcode#3, D-308/309); mojoui.gpu's pixel path was compiled out of it.
- Mac CI round 1 (`scripts/mac-ci gpu`, the macos-26 job now builds `gpu` natively too): wrap refused 614400 bytes (not a multiple of the 16384-byte page) → IOSurfaceAllocSize rounded up to pages. The fractal's kernel didn't compile ("Failed to create compute pipeline state"): the app fell back to the CPU and said why, as designed.
- Rounds 2–3: ci/mac/pixelprobe.mojo, one process per variant (in one process, after the first failure Metal handed out no more memory and every later variant failed too, which misled round 2). Reading any element of a SIMD[float32, 4] kernel argument fails; Float32 → Int alone is fine; the page-sized wrap works (the GPU wrote, the CPU read it back).
- Round 4: Params as four Float32 arguments, rebuilt in the kernel → every variant passes; the app prints "GPU canvas pictures go to the screen without a copy (Metal draws into an IOSurface; Core Animation shows it)", the canvas report says `iosurface 3`, Check on CPU ✓ matches.
- The full set is symmetric top to bottom, so a flipped picture wouldn't show: examples/gpu now opens zoomed on the spiral (zoom 40) for round 5's orientation check against Linux.
- Round 5 (run 42168423382): opened on the spiral, the Mac's picture (IOSurface, `iosurface 2`) matches the Linux one (GTK, GL texture, gpu_copied 2 / cpu_copied 0) side by side: the same orientation and colours; Check on CPU ✓ matches.
