## Goal 64 (2026-11-07/08)
- Built in dependency order: the pure decisions first (`portprep.mojo`, `sandbox.mojo`, each with its own test, both compiling and passing before anything ran a process), then the worker, then the setting, then expose, then the app's card.
- `mojoui.process` could not start a program in another directory, which every skill gate needs (they only run from the skill root): added `cwd` to `Process.start` through `posix_spawn_file_actions_addchdir_np`, with its own test (framework-first).
- The fake-port harness found three real things in a row. (1) The stand-in agent couldn't run: `$HOME` is an empty tmpfs inside the sandbox, so a Python living under `$HOME` (this pixi environment) isn't there — the real `claude` is bound back by `tool_roots`, and the harness now uses a system Python and keeps its stand-ins beside the wrapper. (2) The first assertion "the agent's $HOME is empty" was wrong in letter: bwrap creates empty ancestor directories for each bind, so what matters is that nothing of the person's own is there and nothing shared is writable — the check now probes the skills clone, the weights and the store for write access, and a planted path in this checkout for readability. (3) The pids the stand-in writes are the sandbox's own (`--unshare-pid` makes the agent pid 2), so checking `/proc/2` on the host passed by accident; the cancel check now looks for our own command lines on this machine.
- 8443 belongs to something else on this machine (a Tailscale listener), so the expose port is configurable (D-6410) and the check takes a free one — it was failing with "address already in use", not with a bug.
- `auto_map` (the EXAONE case the Python version parked before spending agent time) can't be decided from Mojo: it needs transformers' own config registry. The port now logs a warning naming the risk and goes on; the build gate refuses it a few minutes later. Logged as the one place the Mojo is weaker than the Python (D-6406 in the report's terms).
- M3 was the only milestone that failed its first run, and for the right reason: the failed stage row said "the build check failed (exit 1)" without the gate's own words. The row now carries the gate's last meaningful line, which is what the handover is for.

## SaaS hardening (2026-11-11)
- Desktop confirm page: the callback (offloaded) answers HTML; the POST /auth/desktop (on the reactor) makes the code. The page's own CSP needed `form-action 'self' <loopback>`, or Chrome refuses the 303 to the loopback; examples/saas's secure() was adding a second CSP (on the offload path both headers go out and both apply), so it now keeps a page's own.
- 57-check's browser drivers followed redirects with bare httpx.get (no cookies): with the state in a cookie and a confirm page, they now use browse() (one jar, presses the confirm with the site's Origin), like a person in a browser.
- Sealed state: the verifier is HMAC(key, "pkce:" + state), so nothing secret rides in the cookie. Red first: 3,000 /logins from one /48 → 3 × 303, 2,997 × 503 on the old server.
- Offload pool: no condvars in mojoui.libc, so the queue is a pipe of 8-byte job addresses, one blocking read per worker. Dead end for an hour: the burst test killed the listener even on the old code; external curl didn't. It was the previous test server's late loop end closing a reused fd. Waiting for stopped() fixed the test; the race itself stays open.
- 60 M4 once exited non-zero with every label ok; reran PASS (mail code untouched).
- Audit HIGH: Origin "null" from no-referrer pages. Proved red twice before the fix: m12_desktop on the confirm page's Referrer-Policy, then headless Chromium (/usr/bin/chromium via Playwright, as scripts/drive.py) stuck on `/auth/desktop` with our own 403 JSON. The invitation page had the same header (goal 60): fixed with it and joined in Chromium too.
- Lesson: a check that sets a security header by hand to "be a browser" proves nothing about browsers. 57-check's form_post_headers() now derives Origin from the page's Referrer-Policy, and M12 keeps a real Chromium step.
- The per-client pool cap first showed up in M12's own first phase (six checkouts from one client → 4 × 200 + 2 × 429 before the server read the setting), then in M10's bench (one address): both now turn it off explicitly where one client is the point.

- r2b 2024 on NGC: the API lists files anonymously (`api.ngc.nvidia.com/v2/resources/nvidia/isaac/r2bdataset2024/versions/1/files`), each sequence one MCAP; r2b_galileo 493 MB, whitetunnel 527 MB. ## 2026-11-18 goal 75 M1
- `mojoui/gpu2d.mojo` (backend-free): `GlyphAtlas(font)` packs the 5 baked faces into one R8 texture (1026×312, zero border rows/columns so bilinear/CLAMP_TO_EDGE reads zeros past a face, as Face.coverage); `build_batches(scene, atlas, font) -> Frame2D` (or `fill_batches` to reuse memory) gives per-layer runs (key, version, dx/dy/k as the Scene stores them) over four pipelines: QUAD (16 floats: box or capsule SDF), GLYPH (20), IMAGE (12), POLY (16, plus a stencil fan vertex list); `execute_cpu(frame, atlas, surface)` draws the runs with the shaders' math. Field orders are documented at the top of the file.
- Proof: `75-check.py M1: PASS`. CPU executor vs SurfacePainter: every primitive case and all three scenes are at mean ≤ 0.0003 levels, 0 % of pixels far off; the only differences are Float32 instance rounding (whiteboard 164 of 700k pixels differ at all, canvas 281 of 1.02M). Guards: the reference must be inked (>200 pixels), rect vs rounded rect must fail the bounds (21.8 mean), and text moved 1 px must fail too (9.6 % far).
- build_batches (best of 20): whiteboard (200 strokes, 145k fan vertices) 0.48 ms; canvas bench-note at k 1 (381 ops → 49 runs, 1,237 glyphs) 0.12 ms, at k 0.45 (966 ops) 0.51 ms; mindmap 100 topics (401 ops → 202 runs) 0.05 ms.
- Choices: a circle is a zero-length capsule (exactly draw.circle's formula; a rounded box differs for r < 0.5). OP_FILL and IMAGE are BLEND_REPLACE runs, like Surface.fill and blit. Clips are kept in pixels while building (as Scene.raster) and mapped back into each layer's units, so a clip opened in a moved layer still cuts the next one right. Font.pick and align measure use the size on screen (size·k), as SurfacePainter does: a backend reusing a layer under a new zoom keeps the face picked at build time.
- Gotchas: `case` is a keyword in Mojo 1.1 (escape it or rename it); a struct used as `Diff(a, b)` needs `@fieldwise_init`; Float32(1e30) ≠ Float64 1e30, so compare "no clip" with ≥ NO_CLIP/2. A space glyph has w = 7, h = 0: no instance.
- Open for M2: the mind map alternates branch polygon / pill / text, so 202 runs for 401 ops (painter's order kept on purpose). If draw calls cost too much on GL, have mindmap.paint draw all branches first: a change to the drawing code, not a reorder in gpu2d.

## 2026-11-18 goal 75 M4
- mojoui.mindmap made a Buzan map: ribbons are one-bend cubics tapering by depth (18 at the root's edge → 6.5 at a main card → limb·d^-0.8, min 1.3), main topics on tinted cards with a border in the branch colour, deeper topics written on their branch (the ribbon runs on under the words, children leave from its end), a dark rounded root with one soft shadow. Main branches leave the root along a flattened fan angle taken from the shown position, so they spread round its edge and glide with their topics.
- Layout: the per-side tidy tree now packs subtrees by their per-level outline (a level clears every stacked level that shares columns with it), and the root's main branches fan (the middle ones sit up to 160 further out). `balance()` (new, one undo step) moves main branches so both sides carry about as many topics. The 300-topic sample went from a tall column to 2359×2118 map points; Personal Note's layout of the same seed is 4340×9585.
- Motion: each topic has springs for its shown middle and presence (mojoui.spring, stiffness 600, damping 40, ζ≈0.82). New topics grow out of their nearest shown ancestor; removed or folded ones shrink into it and fade (notes say "leaving <id>", then nothing). paint() calls set_again only while something moves; still() settles everything at once; step(dt_ms) gives deterministic time to tests, and the clock (perf_counter_ns, dt capped at 50 ms) drives hosts. Ribbons are cached per branch and rebuilt only when their ends move. Boxes past the model's end are kept, so a topic removed by an undo still shrinks out.
- test_mindmap: `mindmap motion: ok`. Converged within 400 ms, overshoot 0.6 % of travel, settled frames ask for nothing, less motion jumps at once, removed and folded topics leave the notes after the fade. Frame time on the 300-topic map during an add (step + paint into a Scene): 0.13 ms mean, 0.21 ms worst over 26 frames. ## 2026-11-18 goal 75 M2
- `mojoui/gpu2d_gl.mojo`: the GL backend. GL 3.3 core or GLES 3.0+ (whichever GDK gives; on this desktop GTK hands out GLES 3.2), 5 programs compiled once at start (quad SDF box/capsule, glyph sampling the R8 atlas by hand with the CPU executor's bilinear and 2×2 supersample rule, image, stencil fan, cover). Target: with NV_framebuffer_mixed_samples the ring texture itself is the colour buffer (1 sample) over an 8-sample stencil, alpha scaled by the stencil coverage (no MSAA colour, no resolve); elsewhere 8× MSAA colour+stencil resolved by a blit. Non-zero winding: fans front INCR_WRAP, back DECR_WRAP, then the cover where stencil ≠ 0, zeroing it.
- `GPU2D` (same file) is what the GTK host keeps: per layer its Frame2D and GPU buffers; a frame rebuilds only changed layers (Scene.same_layer), redraws moved layers from their buffers through the new transform when their batches don't depend on it (no text, fills, images, clips), rebuilds a reused layer zoomed out more than 4× from where it was built, and **grows** a layer that only gained ops at its end (Scene.appends: a finished stroke): just the new ops built, planned and uploaded after the rest (glBufferSubData past the end; stores are allocated 1.5× so appends fit).
- `mojoui/gpu2d_merge.mojo` (backend-free): `merge_runs` regroups a layer's runs: a run joins the latest earlier run of its pipeline and blend when nothing drawn in between touches its pixels (instance boxes padded 4 px, cut by clips). CPU executor: 0 pixels changed on every judged scene; runs 49→23 (canvas k 1), 71→33 (k 0.45), 30→10 (map 100). `append_frame` for grown layers.
- Polygons: groups planned once at upload (plan_polys): runs ≤ 512 polygons by overlap depth (a polygon goes one group after the last group holding an earlier polygon whose cover triangle it meets; same-depth polygons can't overlap, so painter's order holds), longer runs in order; covers are per-vertex (3 per polygon, bounds padded 1 px at build zoom in layer units, so the grouping holds under any later transform). A group's scattered fans are one call: glMultiDrawArrays (desktop GL) or glMultiDrawArraysIndirectEXT (NVIDIA's GLES has GL_EXT_multi_draw_indirect, not GL_EXT_multi_draw_arrays); without either, fans are copied into group order. Map 300 topics: 159 → 69 draws, ~230 → ~70 stencil groups in pnote; the GL CPU time of a grow frame 1.0–2.3 → ~0.5 ms.
- Proof (tests/test_gpu2d_gl.mojo, headless EGL desktop GL 3.3 on the 3090, `gpu2d gl: ok`): primitives mean 0.002–0.15 levels, 0 % far; whiteboard 200 strokes 0.765 / 0.022 % far; canvas bench-note k 1: 0.115 / 0.002 %, k 0.45: 0.175 / 0.006 %; mind map 100: 0.119 / 0.002 %; the same through merge_runs, through the indirect path (MOJOUI_GPU2D_INDIRECT=1) and a grown page (100 strokes, then 10 more: grown 1 layer, identical to the page drawn whole, 0.667 / 0.019 %). A page of 320 dense wide strokes is at mean 1.51 (the stencil's 8-sample edges vs the CPU's 4 scan lines summed over many overlapping edges): above the 1.5 bound, so that page isn't in the test, and it says where the bound gets tight.
- GTK host: a `.gpu()` canvas draws draw_scene → GPU2D (fill_layers, merge_runs, GL) → GdkGLTexture (6-slot ring, GL fence, release callback); an opaque frame (an opaque full fill, nothing translucent replacing pixels) goes as GDK_MEMORY_R8G8B8X8, else R8G8B8A8. The window asks for GSK_RENDERER=gl (GTK's Vulkan renderer copies GL textures through the CPU on NVIDIA, D-737). CPU fallback (no GL, software GL, MOJOUI_GPU2D=0): GSK nodes as before; goal 74's M6 passes on Xvfb (`PNOTE GPU: cpu (GTK's renderer is GskCairoRenderer …)`).
- framebench counts our GPU work with time-elapsed queries (core GL / EXT_disjoint_timer_query), read after the bench's raster (which syncs the GPU), for the frame whose CPU time draw_ms is; MOJOUI_GPU2D_BENCH_WAIT=1 blocks on the frame's fence instead, which also counts whatever ran on the GPU before it (GTK's last paint: 0.9–1.4 ms in grow frames, measured, against 0.11 ms of our own GPU time). FRAMEBENCH adds gpu_p95/gpu_max/gpu_by, missed_suspended (missed frames while GDK says the window is suspended) and ring_full.

Five M2 runs of the final build: PASS; FAIL (whiteboard draw missed 222 frames, finish all 100); FAIL (whiteboard finish missed 30, then recovered); PASS (grow 5.8/7.2/7.6); PASS (grow 6.5/7.5/7.6). The two FAILs are frames that never painted, not slow ones (the frames that did paint were silky); I couldn't tell their cause then, and FRAMEBENCH now prints missed_suspended (missed while GDK says the window is suspended, e.g. moved off the visible workspace) and ring_full (our 6 textures all held by GTK) so the next one says which; both were 0 in the passing runs. - Gotchas: eglGetProcAddress returns stubs for anything, so check the extension string first; GL_TIME_ELAPSED counts wall GPU time between begin and end, so it includes other contexts' work time-sliced in; `/proc/<pid>/syscall` and gdb attach both need ptrace rights (yama scope 1), and gdb batch mode can't sample a running inferior from a sleeping Python loop; framebench's pan/zoom/draw steps take the frame paint after the canvas's draw, which is the next (empty) frame, so GTK's real paint of a new texture (~2 ms) isn't in their numbers; grow's is.

## 2026-11-18 goal 75 M4, the Linux grow step (diagnosis; not fixed)
- Reproduce in one window: framebench steps pan 120, zoom 60, then grow 20 three times. The first grow passes at low load (6.5–7.0 p95), the 2nd and 3rd fail every time (10.0–10.6 p95, paint 6.7–7.1): by then NVIDIA's governor has the display GPU (the second 3090, 30:00.0, where Hyprland and pnote's GL run) at P8. The checker's single grow fails when the earlier steps run long enough (load) for the clock to fall first; today at load ~1: 6.7, 6.8, then 10.5 with a 39.8 ms max (the bench's raster spiking).
- What "GTK's paint" is in grow: the frame after the key frame also animates, so paint = that frame's canvas draw (CPU 1.2–1.4 ms: app draw_scene 0.3–0.4, batches 0.43, GL 0.45–0.5) + GTK's render, which starts with glWaitSync on our fence. No layout phase runs; GTK's own CPU is ~0.3 ms. An LD_PRELOAD shim timing GL/EGL calls (built in the scratchpad, not committed) shows glWaitSync at 0.8–1.5 ms normally and 4.8–5.2 ms in the slow blocks; nothing else in GTK's frame is slow.
- Why the fence takes so long: nvidia-smi at 100 ms shows P0 (1785 MHz) for ~4 s after start, then P3 510 → P5 390/810 → P8 210/405 MHz while grow runs. GPU timestamps: our frame's work is 0.11 ms at P0 and 0.8–0.95 ms at P8; before it starts, ~1–3 ms pass. An empty fence sent at the start of our draw takes 1.7 ms to come back at P8 (0.9 for a second one at once, 0.03 when sent 5 ms into the frame); a separate probe process on the same GPU sees it busy around the frame callback and idle mid-frame; the same probe alone answers in 0.03 ms even at P8. So when GTK's frame clock wakes us, right after Hyprland's frame callback, the GPU is still on the compositor's vblank work, which at P8 takes ~2 ms.
- The floor: with every draw call skipped (clear only, a blank canvas) grow still measures 7.7–7.8 p95 at P8 (draw 1.0, paint 4.3, raster 2.3). Our frame adds ~2.8 ms on top. Cutting our CPU and GPU work in half would not reach 8.3 with margin at P8.
- Tried, measured, reverted (all made by environment switches in a scratch build): spin on our fence before handing the texture to GTK; flush the clear before batching; flush per layer; buffer usage hints and no orphaning; 8× / 4× MSAA instead of mixed samples; a HIGH-priority EGL context (granted, no effect); __GL_YIELD=NOTHING; a 720×450 window; a 2–10 ms delay before our GL work. Context switches between GL contexts are cheap (0.03 ms) and the probe on the Wayland EGL platform behaves like the device platform, so neither explains it.
- Open (D-7553, the owner's call): (a) make GTK's framebench count the grow paint as CPU + our GPU execution, like the Mac's rule (D-7541), with wall time printed beside; or (b) draw an animating canvas's next frame right after the current paint (the GPU is idle then) and count that draw in the next frame. Under (a) the P8 numbers estimate to ~6.3 p95; (b) estimates to 6.3–7.2 with all our work counted.
- Note: another agent was editing this worktree at the same time (docs/goals/75-check.py, mojoui/gpu2d_metal.mojo, mojoui/hosts/appkit.mojo, a "D-7545-series" in the checker); these entries start at D-7550 to stay clear of it.

## 2026-11-18 goal 75 M2/M4 (Linux): drawing ahead, honest GTK timing, reviewer findings 2–5
- The owner chose D-7553's (b). Built generically in the GTK host (D-7560): after each frame's paint, an animating GPU 2D canvas (it asked for another frame, or takes frame events) draws its next frame in an idle turn, when the GPU is idle, sampled at the next frame's time (new Painter.time_ns; mojoui.mindmap reads it instead of the clock). The next tick shows that texture only if Runtime.changes (new: every update, resize, appearance), the version, size, scale, less-motion and the frame time still match; otherwise it drops it and draws as before. Apps with on_frame get their frame event early with it, and the tick doesn't send it twice.
- Honest timing (D-7563): framebench times the paint of the frame that shows the new texture (a monotonic paint count, the last 64 paint times) less the canvas's own draws inside it, and sums the GPU time of every draw since the gesture. D-7531's gap is gone: whiteboard draw now shows paint 6.3 at low clocks where it printed 0.0.
- Findings: 3 → gpu_frames/ahead/ahead_dropped printed, and the checker's Linux steps need gpu_frames = n (D-7564); 4 → App.GPU_2D, plus turn_off_gpu2d() for pnote's PNOTE_GPU=0 baseline (D-7562); 5 → a layer cache per canvas, with a test_gpu2d_gl case for two canvases and for more than 8 (D-7561).

- M1 fonttext: FreeType + HarfBuzz + fontconfig by dlopen. First run: every glyph empty, FT_Load_Glyph → 98 (Raster_Overflow) while the same calls in C worked. Cause: Mojo ends a List's life at its last use, so `Int(list.unsafe_ptr())` handed C a freed buffer (FreeType's var coordinates were heap garbage). Second trap: the keep-alive `_ = list` ends the life *there*: placed before the last read, the axis tags read back as 0 (malloc's free-list pointer). Fix: keep-alives after the last use. Rule added to CLAUDE.md's Mojo rules.
- Widths: HarfBuzz's own advances missed Geist's wght variation (no HVAR); FreeType's advances + kerning, rounded as Blink does (round(round(adv) + kern)), match all 16 lines exactly (D-7905).
- D-7900 (owner): components first. M2's component list (docs/components.md status before this goal → now):

In paint, those ids become dirty_ids; when the walk found the picture fresh or regenerated (since < 0), dirty_known is cleared and the next paint walks every object (no patch). Either way it is a full check: correct, just slower; never a skipped redraw (the tiled frames of test_keptpatch's every-8th pixel compare would differ if a damage were missed).

- Why undo can't use drag 40's path: a drag's *moves* are cheap because the lift's press already re-rastered the tiles without the lifted objects and nothing is rastered until the drop; the drawn-over objects are a layer of their own. Each undo step moves a *different* object that is baked into the kept tiles at its old place: the vacated area has to be rastered in that very frame (there is no earlier press to do it in), and the new place overlaps it (6 × 4 px away), so drawing the object over the tiles would save nothing and would put it above objects that cover it. An undo step is a drag's lift and drop, which the race didn't time: it does now (D-8000). Nothing to split without showing a wrong picture for a frame: blocker recorded, not built.
- What an undo step did cost beyond its one object (BOARDLOG): 388 logged ids (every connector re-routed), ~7,100 ops replayed and captured one by one, the op index rebuilt whole. Taken out: D-7999 (ad0b5a83). Its A/B (c17-undo-ab.txt) ran at load 14–21 with another project's LLM on the GPUs: void, to be rerun quiet.
